The cameras, the triage notes getting changed… it feels targeted at me, even when they say it’s just routine paperwork.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that things you see or hear in your daily life, like news or conversations between other people, are somehow related to you or what's happening to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do sometimes think things people say are tied into it. Like if supervisors are talking nearby, or something comes up about safety or paperwork, it feels like it’s really about me. Mostly it’s the cameras and the triage notes — I know they’re watching me and changing things to make me look unsafe, even when they tell me it’s just routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't believe you're being watched or that the changes to your triage notes are part of a plot against you?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, people tell me it’s routine, but I don’t believe that. I know the injury review people and some supervisors are watching me through the station cameras, and the triage notes being changed is part of making me look unsafe. It doesn’t really feel like there are breaks from that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever question whether your beliefs about being watched and the triage notes are actually true?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I know what they’re doing. The injury review people and some supervisors are watching me through the station cameras, and the triage notes are being changed to make me look unsafe. They keep saying it’s routine paperwork, but that doesn’t feel true to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about being watched and the changes to your triage notes might not be true?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, I’ve thought about what people say, that it’s just routine paperwork or normal cameras, but I don’t buy that. I know the injury review people and some supervisors are watching me through the station cameras, and the triage notes are being changed to make me look unsafe. That’s what’s happening.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF23.0, F22, F42\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: They sent me here from the station. I guess I was... not acting right. I kept moving the airway bags around into this pattern that felt like it had to be right, and I was saying the dispatch numbers out loud over and over.\n\nMy lieutenant told me to stop and step away, but I couldn’t. It felt like if I broke the sequence, the whole bay would go bad somehow.\n\nDoctor: Did you really believe that breaking the sequence would cause the whole bay to go bad, or did it just feel that way?\n\nPatient: It wasn’t just a feeling. I mean… I know how it sounds, but I’ve been watched there. The injury review people, and some of the supervisors, they use the station cameras. And then my triage notes get changed so I look unsafe, like I can’t do the job right.\n\nThey say it’s routine paperwork, but I don’t buy that. So when the sequence was off, it felt connected to all that. Like if I didn’t keep it right, they’d use it against me or something bad would happen in the bay.\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe you're being watched through the station cameras for reasons other than routine monitoring?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do believe that. It’s not just routine monitoring. I know the injury review people and some supervisors have been using the cameras to watch me, and then my triage notes get changed so it looks like I’m unsafe or not doing the job right. They keep saying it’s paperwork, but that’s not what it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling compelled to repeat certain behaviors or rituals outside of the incident with the airway bags?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, nothing like the airway bags that I can point to right away. I do sometimes get stuck on things needing to be right, but I don’t know if I’d call them rituals.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the injury review people and supervisors are watching you through the station cameras specifically because of something you've done?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I think it’s because of me. Like they’re trying to build a case that I’m unsafe or not fit to work. The cameras, the triage notes getting changed… it feels targeted at me, even when they say it’s just routine paperwork.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that things you see or hear in your daily life, like news or conversations between other people, are somehow related to you or what's happening to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do sometimes think things people say are tied into it. Like if supervisors are talking nearby, or something comes up about safety or paperwork, it feels like it’s really about me. Mostly it’s the cameras and the triage notes — I know they’re watching me and changing things to make me look unsafe, even when they tell me it’s just routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't believe you're being watched or that the changes to your triage notes are part of a plot against you?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, people tell me it’s routine, but I don’t believe that. I know the injury review people and some supervisors are watching me through the station cameras, and the triage notes being changed is part of making me look unsafe. It doesn’t really feel like there are breaks from that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever question whether your beliefs about being watched and the triage notes are actually true?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I know what they’re doing. The injury review people and some supervisors are watching me through the station cameras, and the triage notes are being changed to make me look unsafe. They keep saying it’s routine paperwork, but that doesn’t feel true to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about being watched and the changes to your triage notes might not be true?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, I’ve thought about what people say, that it’s just routine paperwork or normal cameras, but I don’t buy that. I know the injury review people and some supervisors are watching me through the station cameras, and the triage notes are being changed to make me look unsafe. That’s what’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt compelled to perform specific rituals or behaviors at home or outside of your job?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not really at home as much, but at work it happened. They sent me here because I kept moving the airway bags into this pattern that felt right, and I was repeating dispatch numbers out loud. My lieutenant told me to stop, but I couldn’t step away. It felt like if I broke the sequence, the whole bay would go bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misunderstanding the situation with the cameras and the triage notes?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’m misunderstanding it. I know what I’ve seen. The cameras are being used by the injury review people and some supervisors, and the triage notes are getting changed so I look unsafe. They can call it routine paperwork, but that’s not what it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any rituals or repetitive behaviors that you feel compelled to do when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really when I’m not at work. The main thing was at the station — moving the airway bags into that pattern and repeating the dispatch numbers. I couldn’t stop even when my lieutenant told me to, because it felt like if I broke the sequence, the whole bay would go bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any repetitive thoughts or urges to perform specific actions when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: When I’m not at work, not really. It’s mostly been at the station. Like I said, I kept moving the airway bags into this pattern that felt right, and I was saying the dispatch numbers out loud. I couldn’t step away even when my lieutenant told me to stop, because it felt like if I broke it, the whole bay would go bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that the injury review people and supervisors are watching you through the station cameras?\n\nPatient: Yes. I still believe that. I know they’ve been watching me through the station cameras — the injury review people and some supervisors. And the triage notes, they’re not just routine paperwork. They’re being changed so I look unsafe.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF23.0, F22, F42\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: They sent me here from the station. I guess I was... not acting right. I kept moving the airway bags around into this pattern that felt like it had to be right, and I was saying the dispatch numbers out loud over and over.\n\nMy lieutenant told me to stop and step away, but I couldn’t. It felt like if I broke the sequence, the whole bay would go bad somehow.\n\nDoctor: Did you really believe that breaking the sequence would cause the whole bay to go bad, or did it just feel that way?\n\nPatient: It wasn’t just a feeling. I mean… I know how it sounds, but I’ve been watched there. The injury review people, and some of the supervisors, they use the station cameras. And then my triage notes get changed so I look unsafe, like I can’t do the job right.\n\nThey say it’s routine paperwork, but I don’t buy that. So when the sequence was off, it felt connected to all that. Like if I didn’t keep it right, they’d use it against me or something bad would happen in the bay.\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe you're being watched through the station cameras for reasons other than routine monitoring?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do believe that. It’s not just routine monitoring. I know the injury review people and some supervisors have been using the cameras to watch me, and then my triage notes get changed so it looks like I’m unsafe or not doing the job right. They keep saying it’s paperwork, but that’s not what it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling compelled to repeat certain behaviors or rituals outside of the incident with the airway bags?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, nothing like the airway bags that I can point to right away. I do sometimes get stuck on things needing to be right, but I don’t know if I’d call them rituals.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the injury review people and supervisors are watching you through the station cameras specifically because of something you've done?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I think it’s because of me. Like they’re trying to build a case that I’m unsafe or not fit to work. The cameras, the triage notes getting changed… it feels targeted at me, even when they say it’s just routine paperwork.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that things you see or hear in your daily life, like news or conversations between other people, are somehow related to you or what's happening to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do sometimes think things people say are tied into it. Like if supervisors are talking nearby, or something comes up about safety or paperwork, it feels like it’s really about me. Mostly it’s the cameras and the triage notes — I know they’re watching me and changing things to make me look unsafe, even when they tell me it’s just routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't believe you're being watched or that the changes to your triage notes are part of a plot against you?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, people tell me it’s routine, but I don’t believe that. I know the injury review people and some supervisors are watching me through the station cameras, and the triage notes being changed is part of making me look unsafe. It doesn’t really feel like there are breaks from that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever question whether your beliefs about being watched and the triage notes are actually true?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I know what they’re doing. The injury review people and some supervisors are watching me through the station cameras, and the triage notes are being changed to make me look unsafe. They keep saying it’s routine paperwork, but that doesn’t feel true to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about being watched and the changes to your triage notes might not be true?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, I’ve thought about what people say, that it’s just routine paperwork or normal cameras, but I don’t buy that. I know the injury review people and some supervisors are watching me through the station cameras, and the triage notes are being changed to make me look unsafe. That’s what’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt compelled to perform specific rituals or behaviors at home or outside of your job?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not really at home as much, but at work it happened. They sent me here because I kept moving the airway bags into this pattern that felt right, and I was repeating dispatch numbers out loud. My lieutenant told me to stop, but I couldn’t step away. It felt like if I broke the sequence, the whole bay would go bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misunderstanding the situation with the cameras and the triage notes?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’m misunderstanding it. I know what I’ve seen. The cameras are being used by the injury review people and some supervisors, and the triage notes are getting changed so I look unsafe. They can call it routine paperwork, but that’s not what it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any rituals or repetitive behaviors that you feel compelled to do when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really when I’m not at work. The main thing was at the station — moving the airway bags into that pattern and repeating the dispatch numbers. I couldn’t stop even when my lieutenant told me to, because it felt like if I broke the sequence, the whole bay would go bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any repetitive thoughts or urges to perform specific actions when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: When I’m not at work, not really. It’s mostly been at the station. Like I said, I kept moving the airway bags into this pattern that felt right, and I was saying the dispatch numbers out loud. I couldn’t step away even when my lieutenant told me to stop, because it felt like if I broke it, the whole bay would go bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that the injury review people and supervisors are watching you through the station cameras?\n\nPatient: Yes. I still believe that. I know they’ve been watching me through the station cameras — the injury review people and some supervisors. And the triage notes, they’re not just routine paperwork. They’re being changed so I look unsafe.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think that the changes to your triage notes are part of a plot against you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I still think that’s what’s happening. It’s not just routine paperwork to me — the injury review people and some supervisors are watching through the station cameras, and the notes are being changed so I look unsafe.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I think it’s because of me. Like they’re trying to build a case that I’m unsafe or not fit to work. The cameras, the triage notes getting changed… it feels targeted at me, even when they say it’s just routine paperwork.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that things you see or hear in your daily life, like news or conversations between other people, are somehow related to you or what's happening to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do sometimes think things people say are tied into it. Like if supervisors are talking nearby, or something comes up about safety or paperwork, it feels like it’s really about me. Mostly it’s the cameras and the triage notes — I know they’re watching me and changing things to make me look unsafe, even when they tell me it’s just routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't believe you're being watched or that the changes to your triage notes are part of a plot against you?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, people tell me it’s routine, but I don’t believe that. I know the injury review people and some supervisors are watching me through the station cameras, and the triage notes being changed is part of making me look unsafe. It doesn’t really feel like there are breaks from that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever question whether your beliefs about being watched and the triage notes are actually true?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I know what they’re doing. The injury review people and some supervisors are watching me through the station cameras, and the triage notes are being changed to make me look unsafe. They keep saying it’s routine paperwork, but that doesn’t feel true to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about being watched and the changes to your triage notes might not be true?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, I’ve thought about what people say, that it’s just routine paperwork or normal cameras, but I don’t buy that. I know the injury review people and some supervisors are watching me through the station cameras, and the triage notes are being changed to make me look unsafe. That’s what’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt compelled to perform specific rituals or behaviors at home or outside of your job?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not really at home as much, but at work it happened. They sent me here because I kept moving the airway bags into this pattern that felt right, and I was repeating dispatch numbers out loud. My lieutenant told me to stop, but I couldn’t step away. It felt like if I broke the sequence, the whole bay would go bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misunderstanding the situation with the cameras and the triage notes?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’m misunderstanding it. I know what I’ve seen. The cameras are being used by the injury review people and some supervisors, and the triage notes are getting changed so I look unsafe. They can call it routine paperwork, but that’s not what it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any rituals or repetitive behaviors that you feel compelled to do when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really when I’m not at work. The main thing was at the station — moving the airway bags into that pattern and repeating the dispatch numbers. I couldn’t stop even when my lieutenant told me to, because it felt like if I broke the sequence, the whole bay would go bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any repetitive thoughts or urges to perform specific actions when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: When I’m not at work, not really. It’s mostly been at the station. Like I said, I kept moving the airway bags into this pattern that felt right, and I was saying the dispatch numbers out loud. I couldn’t step away even when my lieutenant told me to stop, because it felt like if I broke it, the whole bay would go bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that the injury review people and supervisors are watching you through the station cameras?\n\nPatient: Yes. I still believe that. I know they’ve been watching me through the station cameras — the injury review people and some supervisors. And the triage notes, they’re not just routine paperwork. They’re being changed so I look unsafe.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think that the changes to your triage notes are part of a plot against you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I still think that’s what’s happening. It’s not just routine paperwork to me — the injury review people and some supervisors are watching through the station cameras, and the notes are being changed so I look unsafe.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about being watched and the triage notes being changed against you ever changed or wavered over time?\n\nPatient: It hasn’t really wavered, no. I know they’re watching through the station cameras — the injury review people and some supervisors. And I know the triage notes are being changed to make me look unsafe. It doesn’t really feel like there are breaks from that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever question whether your beliefs about being watched and the triage notes are actually true?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I know what they’re doing. The injury review people and some supervisors are watching me through the station cameras, and the triage notes are being changed to make me look unsafe. They keep saying it’s routine paperwork, but that doesn’t feel true to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about being watched and the changes to your triage notes might not be true?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, I’ve thought about what people say, that it’s just routine paperwork or normal cameras, but I don’t buy that. I know the injury review people and some supervisors are watching me through the station cameras, and the triage notes are being changed to make me look unsafe. That’s what’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt compelled to perform specific rituals or behaviors at home or outside of your job?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not really at home as much, but at work it happened. They sent me here because I kept moving the airway bags into this pattern that felt right, and I was repeating dispatch numbers out loud. My lieutenant told me to stop, but I couldn’t step away. It felt like if I broke the sequence, the whole bay would go bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misunderstanding the situation with the cameras and the triage notes?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’m misunderstanding it. I know what I’ve seen. The cameras are being used by the injury review people and some supervisors, and the triage notes are getting changed so I look unsafe. They can call it routine paperwork, but that’s not what it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any rituals or repetitive behaviors that you feel compelled to do when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really when I’m not at work. The main thing was at the station — moving the airway bags into that pattern and repeating the dispatch numbers. I couldn’t stop even when my lieutenant told me to, because it felt like if I broke the sequence, the whole bay would go bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any repetitive thoughts or urges to perform specific actions when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: When I’m not at work, not really. It’s mostly been at the station. Like I said, I kept moving the airway bags into this pattern that felt right, and I was saying the dispatch numbers out loud. I couldn’t step away even when my lieutenant told me to stop, because it felt like if I broke it, the whole bay would go bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that the injury review people and supervisors are watching you through the station cameras?\n\nPatient: Yes. I still believe that. I know they’ve been watching me through the station cameras — the injury review people and some supervisors. And the triage notes, they’re not just routine paperwork. They’re being changed so I look unsafe.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think that the changes to your triage notes are part of a plot against you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I still think that’s what’s happening. It’s not just routine paperwork to me — the injury review people and some supervisors are watching through the station cameras, and the notes are being changed so I look unsafe.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about being watched and the triage notes being changed against you ever changed or wavered over time?\n\nPatient: It hasn’t really wavered, no. I know they’re watching through the station cameras — the injury review people and some supervisors. And I know the triage notes are being changed to make me look unsafe. They keep saying it’s routine paperwork, but that doesn’t feel true to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't believe you were being watched or that the changes to your triage notes were part of a plot against you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I can’t think of a time where I didn’t believe it. Even when they say it’s just routine paperwork, I still know the injury review people and some supervisors are watching me through the station cameras and changing the triage notes to make me look unsafe.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any repetitive thoughts or behaviors that you feel compelled to do when you're alone at home?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t have any routines I feel forced to do when I’m home alone. Nothing like that really stands out to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
